{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01LkRVynBU9mK4wie9q7svE7","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Still 28 lines, last line is ep=20. The ep=30 log hasn't appeared yet. Maybe there IS a buffering issue after all. Even with `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1` and `-u`, the logging module might not be flushing.\n\nActually, wait. Let me rethink. `PYTHONUNBUFFERED` makes stdin/stdout/stderr streams unbuffered at the fd level. But `logging.basicConfig()` creates a `StreamHandler` that writes to `sys.stderr`. When stderr is redirected to a file via `2>&1`, the python `sys.stderr` is set to a file object. The `StreamHandler.emit()` method calls `self.stream.write()` followed by `self.flush()`.\n \nThe logging handler should be flushing each message immediately since `StreamHandler.emit()` explicitly calls flush after writing. Even with file-backed stderr and the shell redirection setup, `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1` should make the file object unbuffered, so messages should appear right away.
